  - src/index.js — minimal Express server with a /health endpoint on port 4000
  - Dockerfile — node:20-alpine, installs deps, copies src, exposes 4000, runs

  project (the hackathon scorer) and this frontend doesn't call any API yet.
  Verified npm install + npm start work and /health returns {"status":"ok"}.
  Docker itself isn't running locally so I couldn't build-test the image, but
  the Dockerfile follows a standard pattern that matches the package layout.

  Let me know if you'd rather this be Python/FastAPI to match the old backend,
  or if you want actual endpoints wired up (e.g. for the ingestion settings /
  chatbot data currently hardcoded in the frontend).

⏺ Good catch — the sidebar's height: 100% in NavBar.css doesn't actually stretch
  to the page's real height because the parent flex container only sets
  min-height (not height), so percentage heights don't resolve against it per
  CSS spec — the sidebar collapses to fit just its content instead. Let me fix
  that.

       45  export default function ChatbotWidget({ accent = '#2f5fe0' }) 
           {
       46    const [open, setOpen] = useState(false)
       47    const [activeIntent, setActiveIntent] = useState(null)
       48 +  const [liveAgentAvailable, setLiveAgentAvailable] = useState
          +(true)                                                        
       49 +  const [stage, setStage] = useState('intents') // intents | c
          +onnecting | waiting | active | ended                          
       50 +  const [liveMessages, setLiveMessages] = useState([])        
       51 +  const [draft, setDraft] = useState('')                      
       52 +  const [endedReason, setEndedReason] = useState(null)        
       53  
       54 +  const wsRef = useRef(null)                                  
       55 +  const sessionIdRef = useRef(null)                           
       56 +                                                              
       57 +  useEffect(() => {                                           
       58 +    if (!open || stage !== 'intents') return                  
       59 +    fetch(`${BACKEND_HTTP_URL}/live-agent/status`)            
       60 +      .then((res) => res.json())                              
       61 +      .then((data) => setLiveAgentAvailable(Boolean(data.enabl
          +ed)))                                                         
       62 +      .catch(() => setLiveAgentAvailable(false))              
       63 +  }, [open, stage])                                           
       64 +                                                              
       65 +  useEffect(() => {                                           
       66 +    return () => {                                            
       67 +      wsRef.current?.close()                                  
       68 +    }                                                         
       69 +  }, [])                                                      
       70 +                                                              
       71 +  function appendLiveMessage(message) {                       
       72 +    setLiveMessages((prev) => [...prev, message])             
       73 +  }                                                           
       74 +                                                              
       75 +  function resetToIntents() {                                 
       76 +    wsRef.current?.close()                                    
       77 +    wsRef.current = null                                      
       78 +    sessionIdRef.current = null                               
       79 +    setStage('intents')                                       
       80 +    setLiveMessages([])                                       
       81 +    setDraft('')                                              
       82 +    setEndedReason(null)                                      
       83 +  }                                                           
       84 +                                                              
       85 +  function connectToLiveAgent() {                             
       86 +    setStage('connecting')                                    
       87 +    setLiveMessages([])                                       
       88 +    const ws = new WebSocket(LIVE_AGENT_WS_URL)               
       89 +    wsRef.current = ws                                        
       90 +                                                              
       91 +    ws.onopen = () => {                                       
       92 +      ws.send(JSON.stringify({ type: 'join', role: 'user' })) 
       93 +    }                                                         
       94 +                                                              
       95 +    ws.onmessage = (event) => {                               
       96 +      const msg = JSON.parse(event.data)                      
       97 +      if (msg.type === 'joined') {                            
       98 +        sessionIdRef.current = msg.sessionId                  
       99 +        setStage('waiting')                                   
      100 +      } else if (msg.type === 'agent_joined') {               
      101 +        setStage('active')                                    
      102 +        appendLiveMessage({ from: 'system', text: 'An agent ha
          +s joined the chat.' })                                        
      103 +      } else if (msg.type === 'message') {                    
      104 +        appendLiveMessage({ from: 'agent', text: msg.text })  
      105 +      } else if (msg.type === 'disconnected') {               
      106 +        setEndedReason(DISCONNECT_REASON_TEXT[msg.reason] || '
          +The live agent chat has ended.')                              
      107 +        setStage('ended')                                     
      108 +        ws.close()                                            
      109 +        wsRef.current = null

⏺ Summary

  Live agent domain (backend) — backend/src/liveAgentDomain.js, wired into
  backend/src/index.js via a WebSocket server at /ws/live-agent alongside the
  existing REST app:
  - Queueing: a user "join" creates a waiting session; connected agents get a
    live queue feed and can accept one.
  - Chat relay between the paired user/agent sockets.
  - Disconnect from either side propagates to the other (disconnected event) and
    cleans up the session.
  - A global enabled toggle (GET/POST /live-agent/status, /live-agent/toggle) —
    turning it off force-disconnects every active session on both ends and
    blocks new users from joining until it's turned back on.

  Frontend:
  - ChatbotWidget now has a live-agent mode: connect, waiting/active/ended
    states, a message thread, and a Disconnect button — plus Zapier-style hover
    pills ("Ask Assistant" / "Chat with Live Agent") that appear above the FAB.
  - New AgentConsole page (/agent-console, linked in the sidebar) — the
    human-agent side: queue list, accept, chat, disconnect, and the on/off
    switch for the whole service.

  Verified end-to-end with a two-browser-context Playwright test: user connects
  → agent sees it queued → accepts → messages exchange both ways → agent flips
  the service off → both sides see "turned off" and disconnect → re-enabled →
  reconnect → user-initiated disconnect also notifies the agent. Also caught and
  fixed a real bug in the process: the agent console kept showing an accepted
  session in the "Waiting" list because the server only pushes queue updates to
  idle agents — fixed by clearing the local queue view on accept.

⏺ One of Gordon's points is a real latent issue, even though it happened not to
  bite us this time: frontend/Dockerfile does COPY . ., which pulls the host's
  node_modules (built for darwin-arm64) into the build context and merges it
  over the container's own linux-installed one — it got lucky here because the
  package sets didn't collide, but that's fragile across machines/architectures.
  Adding .dockerignore fixes it properly.

  context dropped from 43.66MB to 967B and the stack still builds and runs
  clean.

  docker compose up --build.

❯ what is UI localhost server                                                   

⏺ With the docker-compose.yml we set up, the frontend UI is served at:

  http://localhost:5173

  That's nginx inside the frontend container (listening on port 80) mapped to
  host port 5173 ("5173:80" in docker-compose.yml).

  The backend API/WebSocket runs separately at http://localhost:4000 (health
  check at /health, live-agent WebSocket at /ws/live-agent).

  If you're running things without Docker (plain npm run dev in each folder
  instead), the frontend's Vite dev server also defaults to
  http://localhost:5173.

✻ Sautéed for 8s

❯ how to connect the live agent                                                 

⏺ Here's the flow, using the pieces we built:

  1. Start both servers (either via Docker Compose or dev mode):
  # Docker
  docker compose up --build

  # or dev mode (two terminals)
  cd backend && npm run dev     # http://localhost:4000
  cd frontend && npm run dev    # http://localhost:5173

  2. Open the Agent Console (the human-agent side) at:
  http://localhost:5173/agent-console
  - Make sure the "Live agent service" toggle at top-right is On.
  - It sits idle showing "No users waiting."

  3. Open the app as a user at http://localhost:5173/ (or in a second browser
  window/incognito tab, so you're not fighting over one tab):
  - Hover the chat bubble in the bottom-right corner — two pills pop up: "Ask 
    Assistant" and "Chat with Live Agent."
  - Click "Chat with Live Agent." The widget opens and shows "Waiting for an
    agent to join…"

  4. Back in the Agent Console:
  - The session appears under "Waiting (1)" — click Accept.
  - Both sides now show "An agent has joined the chat" and you can type messages
    back and forth.

  5. Disconnecting:
  - Either side can click Disconnect to end that one chat.
  - Flipping the Live agent service toggle to Off in the console
    force-disconnects any active chat on both ends immediately, and blocks new
    users from starting one until it's switched back on.

  One thing to know: it's a real WebSocket connection to
  ws://localhost:4000/ws/live-agent, so both the user tab and the agent console
  tab need the backend actually reachable at that address — if you're running
  the frontend through Docker but the backend elsewhere, update
  VITE_BACKEND_URL/VITE_BACKEND_WS_URL (in docker-compose.yml's build args or a
  .env) to point at wherever the backend actually lives.

  just a restart, since Vite bakes these in at build time).

⏺ What I found

  I tested the actual queue/accept flow directly at the WebSocket protocol level
  and in the browser (both orderings — user connects first, and agent opens the
  console first) and the core logic works correctly in both cases.

  The real gap: AgentConsole.jsx had no error handling at all. If the WebSocket
  can't reach the backend for any reason — backend not running, wrong host/port,
  a stale Docker image that predates this feature, a firewall/network issue —
  the console just sat there silently showing "No users waiting" forever,
  indistinguishable from genuinely having no requests. That's almost certainly
  what you were seeing.

  Fixed: AgentConsole now tracks connection state and shows a clear banner —
  "Could not reach the live agent service at ws://localhost:4000/ws/live-agent. 
  Is the backend running?" — with a Retry button, whenever the socket fails or
  drops. Verified: no banner when backend is healthy, banner appears immediately
  when I kill the backend, and the full connect → accept → chat → disconnect
  flow still passes end-to-end after the fix.

  To debug your specific case, check in this order:
  1. Is the backend actually running and reachable at the URL shown in that
     banner? (curl http://localhost:4000/health)
  2. If using Docker — did you rebuild after the live-agent code was added?

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Found it. Reproduced exactly: when the same browser tab is used to click "Chat
  with Live Agent" and then navigate to /agent-console via the sidebar (the
  natural way to test this solo), the queued session vanishes before the console
  can see it — agent console (same-tab nav) queue panel text: Waiting (0).

  Root cause: App.jsx unmounts ChatbotWidget on the /agent-console route
  ({!isAgentConsole && <ChatbotWidget />}), and ChatbotWidget owns the
  live-agent WebSocket directly — so its cleanup effect closes the socket the
  instant you unmount it, which the backend correctly treats as the user
  disconnecting.

  Fix: lift the WebSocket connection out of ChatbotWidget into a context
  provider mounted at the app root (which never unmounts on route change), so
  navigating to /agent-console in the same tab just hides the widget UI without
  killing the underlying session.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Confirmed working against the actual Docker deployment too.

  Root cause found and fixed

  The queue actually worked correctly server-side in every scenario I tested
  with separate browser tabs/sessions. The real bug: App.jsx unmounted 
  ChatbotWidget whenever the route was /agent-console, and ChatbotWidget owned
  the live-agent WebSocket directly — so the moment you navigated to the Agent
  Console (in the same tab, via the sidebar link, which is the natural way to
  test this solo), React's unmount cleanup closed the socket, which the backend
  correctly read as "the user left." The request vanished from the queue before
  you ever saw it.

  Fix: moved the WebSocket connection out of ChatbotWidget into a
  LiveAgentUserProvider context mounted at the app root (above the router), so
  it's independent of which page is currently rendered. ChatbotWidget is now
  just a UI consumer of that shared state. Navigating to /agent-console still
  hides the widget, but the underlying session stays alive.

  Verified:
  - Same-tab flow (click "Chat with Live Agent" → click "Agent Console" in the
    sidebar) now correctly shows Waiting (1)
  - Full E2E flow (connect → accept → chat both ways → toggle off disconnects
    both → reconnect → manual disconnect) still passes with zero console errors
  - Confirmed against the actual rebuilt Docker Compose stack, not just dev mode

  The stack is running now via docker compose up --build -d — try it again and
  it should reflect correctly regardless of whether you're using one tab or two.

⏺ Summary

  Redesigned the chatbot widget (ChatbotWidget.jsx/.css) to be more attractive
  and interactive:

  Visual
  - Gradient (blue→purple) header and FAB instead of flat color
  - Message avatars — small gradient circles with bot/agent icons next to each
    incoming message, matching the header avatar
  - Header now shows a live status line: "Typically replies instantly" →
    "Connecting…" → "Finding an agent…" → a green pulsing dot + "Connected" →
    "Chat ended"
  - Icons on each help-topic button (plug, warning, refresh) instead of plain
    text rows
  - Pill-shaped message input with a circular gradient send button (paper-plane
    icon), disabled until you type something

  Interaction / motion
  - Smooth pop-in/scale-out panel open/close animation (spring easing) instead
    of an instant show/hide
  - FAB bounces in on load, lifts on hover
  - Animated three-dot typing indicator while connecting/waiting for a live
    agent, instead of static text
  - New messages fade/slide in
  - Unread badge + a pulsing ring around the FAB when a live-agent reply arrives
    while the panel is closed — clears the moment you reopen it
  - Auto-scrolls to the latest message

  Caught and fixed a real layout bug during testing: the new taller header/icons
  pushed the panel over its fixed height, clipping the greeting message almost
  entirely out of view. Fixed by making the panel's max-height responsive to
  viewport size and letting the message body flex naturally instead of
  hard-capping it.

  Verified with the full Playwright regression suite (all existing
  text/selector-based tests still pass, zero console errors) plus fresh
  screenshots of every state — closed/hover, assistant open, waiting with typing
  indicator, active chat, and the unread badge.

⏺ Summary

  "Add New Connection" now opens a real upload flow instead of doing nothing:

  - Click it → a modal opens with a click-or-drag file dropzone
  - Upload a credentials file in CSV (key,value rows, optional header), JSON
    (single object), or .env-style (key=value lines)
  - Parsed fields are previewed with values masked (de••••er) before you commit
    anything
  - Create Connection posts to a new backend endpoint (POST /connections), which
    validates it's a non-empty object and records the connection (only field
    names + a timestamp — the actual credential values are validated and
    discarded, since there's no real third-party API on the other end to hand
    them to)
  - On success, the platform card flips to Enabled and the tab counts update
    live

  Tested all three file formats plus the failure path (unparseable file → clear
  inline error, submit button stays disabled, no crash), verified against the
  actual backend (GET /connections reflects what was created), and confirmed the
  unrelated live-agent flow still passes end-to-end.

  New files: backend/src/connectionsDomain.js,
  frontend/src/credentialFileParser.js,
  frontend/src/components/ConnectionUploadModal.jsx/.css. IngestionSettings.jsx
  now holds platform state locally so a successful upload can flip a card's
  status.

  Monitor" section on it, modeled on your screenshot but improvised with real
  interactive data rather than the flatlined placeholder:

  - Refreshed timestamp, date-range presets (Today/7D/15D/1M/6M/1Y + custom
    start/end inputs) — switching ranges regenerates the series (daily buckets
    for short ranges, weekly for 6M, monthly for 1Y)
  - System card: a checkbox list of 8 metrics doubling as the chart legend, each
    with a live value, a color swatch, and a trend arrow — colored by whether
    that metric's direction is actually good for that metric (e.g., up is good
    for Processed Transactions, down is good for Mismatched Transactions), not
    just a generic red/green
  - Multi-line SVG chart for whatever's checked, with a crosshair + tooltip
    (line-key swatches, values bolded per the interaction spec), and a "View as 
    table" fallback for accessibility
  - Filters row (Organizations/Accounts segmented toggle + Clear) and a list
    panel with mock orgs/accounts, plus Hide/Show Graph

  Followed the dataviz skill throughout: used its validated 8-color categorical
  palette (ran validate_palette.js myself to confirm it passes
  lightness/CVD/contrast checks rather than trusting the doc blindly), 2px lines
  with 8px ringed end-markers, hairline gridlines, crosshair+tooltip
  interaction, and kept series colors separate from status colors (trend arrows
  use the fixed good/critical palette, not the categorical one).

  Caught and fixed a real bug during testing: duplicate React keys in the table
  view (I'd keyed <td>s by date, but every column in a row shares the same date
  — switched to keying by metric id). Verified with Playwright: initial render,

⏺ Summary

  Fixed the parsing bug shown in your screenshot: a 2-row CSV (header row + one
  data row, e.g. TransactionID,Date / 003lg...,2026-08-20) was being read as two
  separate key→value pairs instead of one record with two fields.
  credentialFileParser.js now recognizes that shape and zips the header row
  against the data row correctly.

  Wired up "Manage connection" — previously a no-op — to open a details modal
  showing:
  - Status pill + "Connected since" date
  - Connection fields (masked values, same style as the upload preview) — for
    connections created via upload this session, these are the real parsed
    fields; for the pre-seeded Enabled/Disabled platforms that never went
    through upload, they're plausible mock fields shaped by platform type (SFTP
    gets Host/Port/Username, API gets API Key/Endpoint, others get Client
    ID/Token/Region)
  - Recent transactions table (Transaction ID, date, status) — deterministic
    mock data per platform, so it's the same every time you reopen it rather
    than reshuffling
  - A Disconnect action that reverts the platform back to "Not Connected"

  New files: frontend/src/connectionDetailsData.js (mock data generator),
  frontend/src/components/ConnectionDetailsModal.jsx/.css. IngestionSettings.jsx
  now tracks uploaded credentials per platform so real vs. mock data is used
  appropriately.

  Verified: the exact CSV shape from your screenshot now parses correctly, both
  fresh-upload and pre-seeded connections show data on "Manage connection",
  Disconnect works, and the full existing test suite (live-agent flow,
  connection upload, dashboard) still passes with zero errors.

⏺ Summary

  Data model: connection fields are now consistently stored as an array of 
  objects ([{key, value}, ...]) everywhere — both freshly-uploaded connections
  and the pre-seeded mock ones — instead of the previous mix of plain objects /
  tuple arrays.

  After a successful upload, the app now redirects straight into the Manage 
  connection view for that platform (showing the just-parsed custom fields)
  instead of just closing the modal.

  Manage connection modal now has an Edit button, positioned before Close
  (action order: Disconnect → Edit → Close). Clicking it turns the read-only
  field list into an editable form where you can:
  - Edit any field's value inline
  - Remove a field
  - Add a new custom field (name + value)
  - Or drop in a replacement file to swap out all fields at once (reuses the
    same CSV/JSON/.env parser from the upload flow)

  Save changes POSTs the updated credentials to the backend (POST /connections,
  which already upserts by platform ID) and persists them in front-end state,
  confirmed to survive closing and reopening the modal.

  Verified end-to-end: upload → auto-redirect into Manage → Edit → change a
  value → add a custom field → Save → close → reopen shows the edited data, and
  the backend's /connections reflects the update. Confirmed this works for both
  newly-uploaded connections and the pre-seeded Enabled/Disabled ones. All
  pre-existing flows (live-agent, dashboard, chatbot visibility) still pass —
  two older test scripts needed a one-line update since they encoded the old
  "modal just closes" assumption, which is exactly the behavior this feature
  intentionally changed.

⏺ Summary

  Two things landed this turn:

  1. Chatbot training + support tickets
  - Expanded the knowledge base from 3 to 8 topics (chatbotKnowledge.js), each
    with keywords for lightweight free-text matching — you can now type a
    question into the widget instead of only clicking buttons, with a graceful
    fallback reply when nothing matches
  - The panel now opens straight into a "Popular topics" preview grid plus the
    ask-a-question input
  - When live agent is unavailable, a "Create a support ticket" option appears
    (both in the idle browsing view and after a live chat ends) — fills out a
    subject/message, submits to a new real backend endpoint (POST 
    /support-tickets), and shows a mock success confirmation with a ticket ID,
    assigned CSM name, and "emailed to csm-team@experience.com"
  - Along the way, fixed a real layout bug (the ticket button could get clipped
    outside the panel with no way to scroll to it) and a real staleness bug (the
    widget only rechecked live-agent availability on certain state transitions,
    not when reopened)

  2. Dashboard: expandable per-org/account transaction panels
  - Clicking an Organization or Account row now expands an independent mini
    "System" panel underneath it — its own metric checklist, its own chart, its
    own "Hide Graph" toggle — using deterministic mock data scoped to that
    specific org/account (different orgs show genuinely different numbers,
    scaled by their verified-user count)
  - Added hover/focus tooltips on every metric label (both the main System card
    and the nested panels) showing a plain-English definition, matching the
    reference screenshot's tooltip

  Both features were tested end-to-end with Playwright (including catching and
  fixing the real bugs above), and the full existing regression suite
  (live-agent flow, connection upload/edit, dashboard, chatbot visibility) still
  passes clean.

⏺ Summary

  Support tickets now show up in the Agent Console, pushed live in real time:

  - Backend: liveAgentDomain.js now tracks every connected agent socket (idle or
    mid-chat) and exposes broadcastToAgents(). supportTicketsDomain.js accepts
    an onTicketCreated callback, wired in index.js to broadcast { type: 
    'ticket_created', ticket } to all connected agents the moment a ticket is
    created — reusing the same WebSocket channel agents are already connected
    on, rather than polling.
  - Frontend: AgentConsole.jsx fetches ticket history via GET /support-tickets
    on load, and listens for the new ticket_created WS message to prepend new
    tickets live. A new "Support Tickets" panel below the existing queue/chat
    layout lists each one — subject, ticket ID, message, assigned CSM, and when
    it was created — newest first.

  Verified end-to-end: created a ticket from the chatbot while live agent was
  off, confirmed it appeared in the Agent Console without a page reload, created
  a second one, confirmed both persist after reloading the console (via the GET
  fetch), and ran the full regression suite (live-agent flow, dashboard
  expansion) with zero errors. Also incidentally confirmed the feature already
  works in the wild — there was a real ticket sitting in the console from what
  looks like your own earlier manual testing.

  assistant-local state — the last Q&A shown, the free-text question draft, and

  initial "Hi! What can I help you with?" greeting with the popular-topics list,
  on both Dashboard and Ingestion Settings (it's the same widget everywhere).

  (connecting/waiting/chatting) is not wiped just because you closed the panel —
  that would undo the earlier fix where navigating away shouldn't drop a queued
  or in-progress live chat. Only a session that's already ended gets cleared on
  close, so you don't come back to a stale "chat ended" transcript.

⏺ Now add the normalizeJsonCredentials helper handling both a plain object and

⏺ Update(frontend/src/credentialFileParser.js)
  ⎿  Added 27 lines
      67    return stripEmpty(result);
      68  }
      69  
      70 +// Accepts either a single { field: value } object, or an array
         + of objects to                                                 
      71 +// merge — each array item is either a { key, value } pair (use
         +d verbatim) or                                                 
      72 +// an arbitrary object whose own keys/values are folded in dire
         +ctly, so any                                                   
      73 +// input key names carry straight through to the resulting cred
         +entials.                                                       
      74 +function normalizeJsonCredentials(data) {                      
      75 +  if (Array.isArray(data)) {                                   
      76 +    const merged = {};                                         
      77 +    for (const item of data) {                                 
      78 +      if (typeof item !== "object" || item === null || Array.is
         +Array(item)) {                                                 
      79 +        throw new Error("Each item in the array must be an obje
         +ct of credential fields.");                                    
      80 +      }                                                        
      81 +      const keys = Object.keys(item);                          
      82 +      if (keys.length === 2 && keys.includes("key") && keys.inc
         +ludes("value")) {                                              
      83 +        merged[item.key] = item.value;                         
      84 +      } else {                                                 
      85 +        Object.assign(merged, item);                           
      86 +      }                                                        
      87 +    }                                                          
      88 +    return stripEmpty(merged);                                 
      89 +  }                                                            
      90 +                                                               
      91 +  if (typeof data !== "object" || data === null) {             
      92 +    throw new Error("The JSON must be an object, or an array of
         + objects, of credential fields.");                             
      93 +  }                                                            
      94 +  return stripEmpty(data);                                     
      95 +}                                                              
      96 +                                                               
      97  function stripEmpty(obj) {
      98    const result = {};
      99    for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(obj)) {

⏺ Summary

  In the Manage Connection modal's Edit mode, there are now two import options
  side by side: "Replace all fields from a file…" (existing) and "Upload plain 
  text…" (new). The new one opens a textarea where you can paste:

  - A plain JSON object — {"Host": "...", "Port": "22"}
  - An array of {key, value} pairs — [{"key": "ClientID", "value": "..."}]
  - An array of arbitrary objects — [{"TransactionID": "..."}, {"Region": "...",
    "Environment": "prod"}] — each object's own keys get folded straight in, so
    it works regardless of shape
  - Plain input values — key=value lines or key,value rows (reuses the existing
    non-JSON parser)

  Clicking "Parse & apply" populates the editable field list from whatever key
  names were in the input; "Save changes" persists it to the backend exactly
  like the other edit paths.

  Under the hood, credentialFileParser.js's JSON detection now sniffs content
  (leading {/[) instead of only trusting a .json filename, since pasted text has
  no filename — this also makes the original file-upload flow slightly more
  robust as a side effect. Added normalizeJsonCredentials to handle the
  object-vs-array-of-objects merge logic.

  Verified all four input shapes end-to-end (parse → apply → save → persists on
  reopen), and ran the full regression suite — while at it, found and fixed two
  other test scripts left over from an earlier session that still assumed the
  old "modal closes after upload" behavior (superseded by the
  redirect-into-Manage-view feature from a prior turn); everything now passes
  clean.
